=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
There is a printing machine that prints line segments on the xy-plane by emitting a laser.

- At the start of printing, the laser position is at coordinate (0, 0).
- 
When printing a line segment, the procedure below is followed.

- First, move the laser position to one of the endpoints of the line segment.
- One may start drawing from either endpoint.


- Then, move the laser position in a straight line from the current endpoint to the other endpoint while emitting the laser.
- It is not allowed to stop printing in the middle of a line segment.




- 
When not emitting the laser, the laser position can move in any direction at a speed of S units per second.

- When emitting the laser, the laser position can move along the line segment being printed at a speed of T units per second.
- The time required for operations other than moving the laser position can be ignored.

Takahashi wants to print N line segments using this printing machine.
The i-th line segment connects coordinates (A_i, B_i) and (C_i, D_i).
Some line segments may overlap, in which case he needs to print the overlapping parts for each line segment separately.
What is the minimum number of seconds required to complete printing all the line segments when he operates the printing machine optimally?

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
N S T
A_1 B_1 C_1 D_1
\vdots
A_N B_N C_N D_N

Output

Print the answer.
Your output will be considered correct if the absolute or relative error from the true value does not exceed 10^{-6}.

Constraints


- All input values are integers.
- 1 \le N \le 6
- 1 \le T \le S \le 1000
- -1000 \le A_i,B_i,C_i,D_i \le 1000
- (A_i,B_i) \neq (C_i,D_i) ( 1 \le i \le N )

Sample Input 1

3 2 1
1 3 2 1
0 2 0 0
3 0 2 0

Sample Output 1

6.44317475868633722080


- Emit the laser while moving the laser position from (0,0) to (0,2), printing the second line segment.
- This takes 2 seconds.


- Move the laser position from (0,2) to (1,3) without emitting the laser.
- This takes \sqrt{2}/2 seconds.


- Emit the laser while moving the laser position from (1,3) to (2,1), printing the first line segment.
- This takes \sqrt{5} seconds.


- Move the laser position from (2,1) to (2,0) without emitting the laser.
- This takes 1/2 second.


- Emit the laser while moving the laser position from (2,0) to (3,0), printing the third line segment.
- This takes 1 second.


- The total time taken is 2 + (\sqrt{2}/2) + \sqrt{5} + (1/2) + 1 \approx 6.443175 seconds.

Sample Input 2

2 1 1
0 0 10 10
0 2 2 0

Sample Output 2

20.97056274847714058517

Sample Input 3

6 3 2
-1000 -1000 1000 1000
1000 -1000 -1000 1000
-1000 -1000 1000 1000
1000 -1000 -1000 1000
1000 1000 -1000 -1000
-1000 1000 1000 -1000

Sample Output 3

9623.35256169626864153344

Multiple line segments overlap here, and you need to print the overlapping parts for each line segment separately.

Sample Input 4

6 10 8
1000 1000 -1000 -1000
1000 -1000 -1000 -1000
-1000 1000 1000 1000
-1000 1000 -1000 -1000
1000 1000 1000 -1000
1000 -1000 -1000 1000

Sample Output 4

2048.52813742385702910909

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Try to avoid patterns like these:
- spending too long comparing nearby plans before committing to one workable approach;
- returning from code to broad algorithm discussion without a concrete contradiction;
- turning one local doubt into a full restart when the current draft only needs a small patch.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Let's draft code:

import sys, itertools, math

def main():
    data=sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data: return
    it=iter(data)
    N=int(next(it)); S=int(next(it)); T=int(next(it))
    segs=[]
    total_print=0.0
    for _ in range(N):
        A=int(next(it)); B=int(next(it)); C=int(next(it)); D=int(next(it))
        p=(A,B); q=(C,D)
        segs.append((p,q))
        total_print += math.hypot(C-A, D-B) / T
    # precompute distances between endpoints? 
    # For each segment i, endpoints p_i, q_i. For orientation bit 0: start p, end q; bit 1: start q, end p.
    # Need travel distance: from current pos to start endpoint.
    # Could compute on fly.
    min_travel = float('inf')
    # maybe use list of endpoints arrays
    starts = []
    ends = []
    for p,q in segs:
        starts.append((p,q)) # start if bit0 p, bit1 q
        ends.append((q,p)) # end if bit0 q, bit1 p
    # But easier:
    for perm in itertools.permutations(range(N)):
        # perm is tuple of segment indices
        for mask in range(1<<N):
            # current position starts at (0,0)
            cx=cy=0.0
            travel=0.0
            # For each position in perm, orientation bit = (mask>>pos)&1? Need align with segment index in perm.
            # If bit 0: start p, end q; bit 1: start q, end p.
            for pos, idx in enumerate(perm):
                p,q=segs[idx]
                if (mask>>pos)&1:
                    sx,sy=q; ex,ey=p
                else:
                    sx,sy=p; ex,ey=q
                travel += math.hypot(cx-sx, cy-sy)
                cx,cy=ex,ey
            if travel < min_travel: min_travel=travel
    ans = total_print + min_travel / S
    print("{:.15f}".format(ans))

Now, let's maybe implement with precomputed endpoints for speed and clarity:
import sys, math, itertools

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data: return
    N = int(data[0]); S = int(data[1]); T = int(data[2])
    segs = []
    total_print = 0.0
    idx=3
    for _ in range(N):
        A=int(data[idx]); B=int(data[idx+1]); C=int(data[idx+2]); D=int(data[idx+3]); idx +=4
        p=(A,B); q=(C,D)
        segs.append((p,q))
        total_print += math.hypot(C-A, D-B) / T
    starts0=[]; ends0=[]; starts1=[]; ends1=[]
    for p,q in segs:
        starts0.append(p); ends0.append(q)
        starts1.append(q); ends1.append(p)
    min_travel = float('inf')
    for perm in itertools.permutations(range(N)):
        for mask in range(1<<N):
            cx=0.0; cy=0.0; travel=0.0
            for pos, idx in enumerate(perm):
                if (mask >> pos) & 1:
                    sx,sy = starts1[idx]
                    ex,ey = ends1[idx]
                else:
                    sx,sy = starts0[idx]
                    ex,ey = ends0[idx]
                travel += math.hypot(cx - sx, cy - sy)
                cx,cy = ex,ey
            if travel < min_travel:
                min_travel = travel
    ans = total_print + min_travel / S
    print("{:.20f}".format(ans))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

However, let's think if there is any hidden issue with brute force over permutations and orientations: It assumes each segment printed exactly once, in a total order. Is there any possibility to print a segment, then later print another segment, then later print the first segment again? No, each segment once. Any valid schedule can be represented as an order of the N segments, because each segment is printed continuously and cannot be interleaved. Since only one laser, operations are sequential. Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any possibility to print a segment while moving from one endpoint to the other, but if the segment is not straight? Input line segment connects two coordinates, straight. Good.

Our total_print sum lengths/T. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible better approach using DP over subsets and last segment/orientation to avoid permutations. But brute force is simpler and less error-prone. However, let's think if there is any hidden issue with brute force over permutations and orientations: It assumes each segment printed exactly once, in a total order. Is there any possibility to print a segment, then later print another segment, then later print the first segment again? No, each segment once. Any valid schedule can be represented as an order of the N segments, because each segment is printed continuously and cannot be interleaved. Since only one laser, operations are sequential. Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "One may start drawing from either endpoint" - This means for each segment, orientation can be chosen independently. Our mask.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "The time required for operations other than moving the laser position can be ignored." This includes turning laser on/off, so no penalty for stopping and starting. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "When emitting the laser, the laser position can move along the line segment being printed at a speed of T units per second." If T is less than S, printing takes longer than moving same distance. But fixed. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "Some line segments may overlap, in which case he needs to print the overlapping parts for each line segment separately." Could this mean if two segments overlap, you cannot print them consecutively in one pass even if endpoints align because the overlapping part would be printed twice? Wait "needs to print the overlapping parts for each line segment separately" means he must print them separately, i.e., two passes. Our model does two passes. If endpoints align and one segment is a subsegment of another, can you print the longer segment, then the shorter segment? Yes, two passes. Our model.

Now, let's think about if there is any possibility to print a segment while moving from one endpoint to the other, and if the path overlaps with a previously printed segment, that's okay. No effect.

But travel is float. math.hypot accepts ints. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible better approach using DP over subsets and last segment/orientation to avoid permutations. But brute force is simpler and less error-prone. However, let's think if there is any hidden issue with brute force over permutations and orientations: It assumes each segment printed exactly once, in a total order. Is there any possibility to print a segment, then later print another segment, then later print the first segment again? No, each segment once. Any valid schedule can be represented as an order of the N segments, because each segment is printed continuously and cannot be interleaved. Since only one laser, operations are sequential. Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

Is there any possibility to print a segment, then later print another segment, then later print the first segment again? No, each segment once. Any valid schedule can be represented as an order of the N segments, because each segment is printed continuously and cannot be interleaved. Since only one laser, operations are sequential. Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any possibility to print a segment while moving from one endpoint to the other, but if the segment is not straight? Input line segment connects two coordinates, straight. Good.

Our total_print sum lengths/T. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible better approach using DP over subsets and last segment/orientation to avoid permutations. But brute force is simpler and less error-prone. However, let's think if there is any hidden issue with brute force over permutations and orientations: It assumes each segment printed exactly once, in a total order. Is there any possibility to print a segment, then later print another segment, then later print the first segment again? No, each segment once. Any valid schedule can be represented as an order of the N segments, because each segment is printed continuously and cannot be interleaved. Since only one laser, operations are sequential. Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "The time required for operations other than moving the laser position can be ignored." This includes turning laser on/off, so no penalty for stopping and starting. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "When emitting the laser, the laser position can move along the line segment being printed at a speed of T units per second." If T is less than S, printing takes longer than moving same distance. But fixed. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "Some line segments may overlap, in which case he needs to print the overlapping parts for each line segment separately." Could this mean if two segments overlap, you cannot print them consecutively in one pass even if endpoints align because the overlapping part would be printed twice? Wait "needs to print the overlapping parts for each line segment separately" means he must print them separately, i.e., two passes. Our model does two passes. If endpoints align and one segment is a subsegment of another, can you print the longer segment, then the shorter segment? Yes, two passes. Our model.

Now, let's think about if there is any possibility to print a segment while moving from one endpoint to the other, and if the path overlaps with a previously printed segment, that's okay. No effect.

However, let's think if there is any hidden issue with brute force over permutations and orientations: It assumes each segment printed exactly once, in a total order. Is there any possibility to print a segment, then later print another segment, then later print the first segment again? No, each segment once. Any valid schedule can be represented as an order of the N segments, because each segment is printed continuously and cannot be interleaved. Since only one laser, operations are sequential. Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any possibility to print a segment while moving from one endpoint to the other, but if the segment is not straight? Input line segment connects two coordinates, straight. Good.

Our total_print sum lengths/T. Good.

However, let's think if there is any hidden issue with brute force over permutations and orientations: It assumes each segment printed exactly once, in a total order. Is there any possibility to print a segment, then later print another segment, then later print the first segment again? No, each segment once. Any valid schedule can be represented as an order of the N segments, because each segment is printed continuously and cannot be interleaved. Since only one laser, operations are sequential. Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

Could this mean if two segments overlap, you cannot print them consecutively in one pass even if endpoints align because the overlapping part would be printed twice? Wait "needs to print the overlapping parts for each line segment separately" means he must print them separately, i.e., two passes. Our model does two passes. If endpoints align and one segment is a subsegment of another, can you print the longer segment, then the shorter segment? Yes, two passes. Our model.

Is there any possibility to print a segment, then later print another segment, then later print the first segment again? No, each segment once. Any valid schedule can be represented as an order of the N segments, because each segment is printed continuously and cannot be interleaved. Since only one laser, operations are sequential. Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any possibility to print a segment while moving from one endpoint to the other, but if the segment is not straight? Input line segment connects two coordinates, straight. Good.

Our total_print sum lengths/T. Good.

However, let's think if there is any hidden issue with brute force over permutations and orientations: It assumes each segment printed exactly once, in a total order. Is there any possibility to print a segment, then later print another segment, then later print the first segment again? No, each segment once. Any valid schedule can be represented as an order of the N segments, because each segment is printed continuously and cannot be interleaved. Since only one laser, operations are sequential. Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

Could this mean if two segments overlap, you cannot print them consecutively in one pass even if endpoints align because the overlapping part would be printed twice? Wait "needs to print the overlapping parts for each line segment separately" means he must print them separately, i.e., two passes. Our model does two passes. If endpoints align and one segment is a subsegment of another, can you print the longer segment, then the shorter segment? Yes, two passes. Our model.

Is there any possibility to print a segment, then later print another segment, then later print the first segment again? No, each segment once. Any valid schedule can be represented as an order of the N segments, because each segment is printed continuously and cannot be interleaved. Since only one laser, operations are sequential. Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

However, let's think if there is any hidden issue with brute force over permutations and orientations: It assumes each segment printed exactly once, in a total order. Is there any possibility to print a segment, then later print another segment, then later print the first segment again? No, each segment once. Any valid schedule can be represented as an order of the N segments, because each segment is printed continuously and cannot be interleaved. Since only one laser, operations are sequential. Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

Could this mean if two segments overlap, you cannot print them consecutively in one pass even if endpoints align because the overlapping part would be printed twice? Wait "needs to print the overlapping parts for each line segment separately" means he must print them separately, i.e., two passes. Our model does two passes. If endpoints align and one segment is a subsegment of another, can you print the longer segment, then the shorter segment? Yes, two passes. Our model.

Is there any possibility to print a segment, then later print another segment, then later print the first segment again? No, each segment once. Any valid schedule can be represented as an order of the N segments, because each segment is printed continuously and cannot be interleaved. Since only one laser, operations are sequential. Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

Is there any possibility to print a segment, then later print another segment, then later print the first segment again? No, each segment once. Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "move the laser position to one of the endpoints of the line segment" - If the current position is already at an endpoint, moving to that endpoint takes 0. If the current position is at the other endpoint, and you choose to start at the current endpoint, travel 0. Our orientation bit chooses start endpoint; if current equals start, distance 0. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "One may start drawing from either endpoint" - This means for each segment, orientation can be chosen independently. Our mask.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "When emitting the laser, the laser position can move along the line segment being printed at a speed of T units per second." If T is less than S, printing takes longer than moving same distance. But fixed. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "Some line segments may overlap, in which case he needs to print the overlapping parts for each line segment separately." Could this mean if two segments overlap, you cannot print them consecutively in one pass even if endpoints align because the overlapping part would be printed twice? Wait "needs to print the overlapping parts for each line segment separately" means he must print them separately, i.e., two passes. Our model does two passes. If endpoints align and one segment is a subsegment of another, can you print the longer segment, then the shorter segment? Yes, two passes. Our model.

Now, let's think about if there is any possibility to print a segment while moving from one endpoint to the other, and if the path overlaps with a previously printed segment, that's okay. No effect.

However, let's think if there is any hidden issue with brute force over permutations and orientations: It assumes each segment printed exactly once, in a total order. Is there any possibility to print a segment, then later print another segment, then later print the first segment again? No, each segment once. Any valid schedule can be represented as an order of the N segments, because each segment is printed continuously and cannot be interleaved. Since only one laser, operations are sequential. Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible better approach using DP over subsets and last segment/orientation to avoid permutations. But brute force is simpler and less error-prone. However, let's think if there is any hidden issue with brute force over permutations and orientations: It assumes each segment printed exactly once, in a total order. Is there any possibility to print a segment, then later print another segment, then later print the first segment again? No, each segment once. Any valid schedule can be represented as an order of the N segments, because each segment is printed continuously and cannot be interleaved. Since only one laser, operations are sequential. Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible better approach using DP over subsets and last segment/orientation to avoid permutations. But brute force is simpler and less error-prone. However, let's think if there is any hidden issue with brute force over permutations and orientations: It assumes each segment printed exactly once, in a total order. Is there any possibility to print a segment, then later print another segment, then later print the first segment again? No, each segment once. Any valid schedule can be represented as an order of the N segments, because each segment is printed continuously and cannot be interleaved. Since only one laser, operations are sequential. Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "One may start drawing from either endpoint" - This means for each segment, orientation can be chosen independently. Our mask.

Now, let's consider if there is any issue with "The time required for operations other than moving the laser position can be ignored." This includes turning laser on/off, so no penalty for stopping and starting. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "When emitting the laser, the laser position can move along the line segment being printed at a speed of T units per second." If T is less than S, printing takes longer than moving same distance. But fixed. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is any issue with "Some line segments may overlap, in which case he needs to print the overlapping parts for each line segment separately." Could this mean if two segments overlap, you cannot print them consecutively in one pass even if endpoints align because the overlapping part would be printed twice? Wait "needs to print the overlapping parts for each line segment separately" means he must print them separately, i.e., two passes. Our model does two passes. If endpoints align and one segment is a subsegment of another, can you print the longer segment, then the shorter segment? Yes, two passes. Our model.

Now, let's think about if there is any possibility to print a segment while moving from one endpoint to the other, and if the path overlaps with a previously printed segment, that's okay. No effect.

But travel is float. math.hypot accepts ints. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is a possible better approach using DP over subsets and last segment/orientation to avoid permutations. But brute force is simpler and less error-prone. However, let's think if there is any hidden issue with brute force over permutations and orientations: It assumes each segment printed exactly once, in a total order. Is there any possibility to print a segment, then later print another segment, then later print the first segment again? No, each segment once. Any valid schedule can be represented as an order of the N segments, because each segment is printed continuously and cannot be interleaved. Since only one laser, operations are sequential. Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

Is there any possibility to print a segment, then later print another segment, then later print the first segment again? No, each segment once. Any valid schedule can be represented as an order of the N segments, because each segment is printed continuously and cannot be interleaved. Since only one laser, operations are sequential. Could there be a schedule where between printing segment A and B, you move to an endpoint of A and print A again? No need. So yes.

Our total_print sum lengths/T. Good.
